Rejected: a mutable ThreadLocal-style `set`/`get` (hidden mutation, values that outlive their step); an explicit context parameter (every signature carries it).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 2026-09-25 | A `Duration` on the wire (§9.12)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | **`"90.5s"` by default, any of five on request** (user: "90.5s, but we need to be able to convert to different"). `DurationStyle { Seconds, Iso8601, Text, Nanos, Millis }` is the format's policy, like `EnumStyle`. Spec D60 addendum. Rejected: ISO 8601 by default (Go/Python stdlib do not read it), seconds as a number (precision).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| 2026-09-25 | Identifiers and invisible characters (notes #21)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | **UAX #31** (user's choice over the recommended fixed list): identifiers are `XID_Start XID_Continue*` plus `_`, so a bidi control, a zero-width character or a no-break space is no longer an identifier byte — the Trojan-source case (CVE-2021-42574) becomes a diagnostic. Costs Unicode tables in the lexer, and in the self-hosted one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 2026-09-25 | Changing a by-value struct parameter (R20 follow-up 1)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | **A warning**: a function that assigns a `var` field of a value-struct parameter, directly or through a method that writes `self`, is told the caller never sees it, with `*T` or returning the value as the fixes. R20's rule (`val`/`var` govern rebinding only) is unchanged. Rejected: Swift's immutable parameters (changes R20).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| 2026-09-25 | Guard binding (I1)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | **Let-else plus `??` on Result** (user, after `archive/veles-guard-design.md`): `val x = r else { e => ... }` binds or leaves (Result, nullable, variant pattern; one-statement `else return` allowed); `r ?? fallback` / `r ?? { e => ... }` is `?:` for a Result, and each operator on the other kind is an error with a fix that swaps it. Spec D61. Rejected: widening `?:` to Result (the recommendation), a `Fallible` trait (its own decision, later).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 2026-09-26 | Fewer panics: prove the index (user: "atOrPanic is kind of a cheatcode")                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **All four parts, in order A → B → C, D alongside.** A: list patterns in `when` and let-else (`val [h, p, s] = xs else ...`, `[x, ..rest]`). B: bounds facts — `xs.at(i)` is `T` where the checker knows the index is in range (index loops, `i < len` guards, constant indexes after a length check; a `MutableList`'s facts end at any call that could reach it). C: `atOrPanic`/`getOrPanic`/`refOrPanic` removed; the only way left is `?: panic("why")` (user's choice over `.expect("why")` and keeping the method with a lint). D: `indices()`, `splitOnce`, `enumerate`/`zip` on List. Spec D62. Built: **A** (2026-09-26: parser, checker, `list.slice` runtime op for `..rest`, examples/features/listpatterns, docs 04, std/jwt + std/http request line migrated). **B** (2026-09-26: sema/bounds.go — facts in the smart-cast map; `indices()`; `?:` after a proven read is a warning with a fix; ~20 std sites now plain `at`, the rest are index arithmetic or `self` with calls; found D63 on the way). **C** (2026-09-26: the three names are errors whose fix writes `(x.at(i) ?: panic("TODO: say why this cannot fail"))`; std, examples, bench, docs and tests migrated by hand — list patterns, iteration and `?: return` where they fit, a stated invariant elsewhere (one helper per data structure); codegen now inlines list `len` and the bounds-checked element read: sha256 301→72 ms, json 314→135 ms, sort 295→108 ms). **D** (2026-09-26): `indices()`, `s.splitOnce(sep): (string, string)?`, eager `List.enumerate(): List<(i64, T)>` (`zip` was already on List); splitOnce replaced the hand-split header, target, query, ISO-duration and HTTP-date code in std. |
| 2026-09-26 | A `catch` block for panics                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | **Not added; D52 stands** (user: "we use gather"). Panics are the bug channel, not exceptions: they stay unrecoverable inside a task and are observed only where a task ends (`gather`). Presented: function-level `fun f() { … } catch { p => … }` (recommended), a catch after any block, or none. Should it be revisited, the panic is bound with the D61 handler form `catch { p => … }`, not `this`, since `this` would hide a method's receiver.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| 2026-09-26 | `self` becomes `this` (user)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | **Built 2026-09-26.** The receiver is spelled `this` across the language, std, docs and tooling (spec D65, v0.40). `self` still parses as the receiver with an error whose fix writes `this` (`veles check --fix`, LSP quick fix, inside interpolations too); 1,520 sites migrated by a token-level rewrite (code only, never comments or string text), prose by hand; `Self` the type is unchanged; the VS Code grammar highlights `this`, flags `self`, and no longer marks `enum`/`for` illegal.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| 2026-09-26 | A MutableList where a List is expected (found by D62)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | **Moved when unescaped, otherwise `.toList()`** (user, recommended of four): the checker passed the same handle, contradicting D35 (a List could shrink during a call, or be shared with a task while written). Now a fresh local that never escaped converts at its last use with no copy; anything else is an error with a `.toList()`/`.toMap()`/`.toSet()` fix. Rejected: implicit copy, always explicit, keeping the view. Spec D63. Built 2026-09-26 (sema/move.go; `==` and compiler-written code compare without converting; std: 4 `.toList()` copies, the sha leftover buffer and crypto padding).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| 2026-09-26 | A Kotlin-style `x!!` to panic on null (user: "controversial, so I'm not sure")                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | **Not added; every panic shows its location instead** (user, recommended of four): `!!` would be D62's cheatcode at two characters, for every `T?`, with no reason recorded. What it offered was the line, so panics now print `at file:line:col` (relative to the package root) under the message — `panic(...)`, overflow, division, `pow`, non-exhaustive match, list index; kept through `scope`'s re-raise, `Panic.location` at `gather`, printed by `veles test`. Rejected: `!!` everywhere, `!!` with a warning + fix, `!!` only in tests. Spec D64. Built 2026-09-26 (runtime `veles_panic_at`, `veles_list_index_panic`; codegen `g.where`). Known gap: a std panic for caller misuse (`swap`) reports the std line.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| 2026-09-26 | Executor threading model (§9.9)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | **Work-stealing M:N over one shared heap** (user, recommended of three): tasks may resume on any worker; GC stops workers at safepoints (allocation, suspension); per-worker allocation buffers; real `Mutex`/`Atomic`; thread-safe channels/timers/poller. Staged: global queue first, stealing second, each measured in `bench/`. A module-level `var` that is not `Mutex`/`Atomic` becomes an error. Spec D66. Rejected: thread-per-core (imbalance, still needs safepoints), single thread + offload pool (breaks D35). _Both stages built 2026-09-27._                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| 2026-09-26 | FFI shape (§9.8)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | **Hand-written `extern` blocks in any package + a `[native]` table in `veles.toml`** (`libs`, `pkg-config`, Windows lib paths), `extern struct`, C function-pointer types for callbacks; "maybe" a `veles bindgen` later that writes the same form (user). User asked that every extern be unsafe — it already is: a call needs `unsafe`, and an extern cannot be taken as a value. Spec D67. Rejected: `@cImport`-style header import. _Not built._                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| 2026-09-26 | Signals and graceful shutdown                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | **A signal is awaited** (user, recommended of three): `os.shutdownSignal(): os.Signal` suspends until SIGINT/SIGTERM (Ctrl+C/Break/close on Windows), installs its handlers only when first asked, a second signal has the default effect; `http.serve(..., stop:, grace:)` stops accepting, drains, cancels after `grace`. Spec D68. Rejected: every round (tight loops 3–9× slower than C); an `@unpolled` annotation (blocks collections, uncancellable); signal preemption as Go (a redesign). |
| 2026-10-28 | F18: precision for `f64`/`f32.toFixed` | **Dynamic precision with no arbitrary small cap; reject negative or unrepresentable output sizes; spec D219** (user: "the C or go / rust /java /kotlin way"). Rejected: the silent 0–30 clamp; JavaScript's explicit 0–100 cap. |
| 2026-10-28 | Q66 + Q67: standard-input text and byte readers | **Checked text readers plus raw-byte variants; `readLine`/`readLineBytes` default to 64 KiB, `readAll`/`readAllBytes` to 1 MiB, with caller overrides; text rejects invalid UTF-8 as `IoError(InvalidData)`, over-limit reads throw `TooLong`; spec D220** (user, selected checked text plus bytes and bounded defaults). Rejected: byte-only existing readers, unbounded defaults, and required limits with no default. |
| 2026-10-29 | Q68: common byte-size units | **A single `std/bytes` module of `i64` IEC constants (`B`, `KiB`, `MiB`, `GiB`, `TiB`, `PiB`, `EiB`), used across standard-library byte sizes; spec D221** (user: "IEC, not decimal" and "one, not separates"). Rejected: an enum, ambiguous decimal `KB`/`MB`, separate per-module constants, and a `ByteSize` wrapper for now. |
| 2026-10-29 | Q69: zero-limit behavior for `Regex.split` | **`limit == 0` returns no pieces; positive limits bound the result and keep the remainder in the last piece; negative limits remain unlimited; spec D222** (user selected recommended of 3). Rejected: zero as unlimited (duplicates the negative sentinel) and one unsplit piece at zero (contradicts the documented bound). |
| 2026-10-29 | Q70: shared Unicode tables and code-point API (F3) | **One generated public `std/unicode` module** with common predicates and general-category/script lookup, using the pinned Go Unicode version; `std/regex` and the self-hosted lexer share its data, while regex simple folding remains internal; spec D223 (user selected recommended of 3). Rejected: internal-only tables with no user API; leaving the generated datasets separate. |
| 2026-10-29 | Q71: shared diagnostic quoting API (F4) | **Public `fmt.quote` with Go-style escaping** for quotes, backslashes, controls and non-printable Unicode (D223); TOML, version and selfhost use it, while JSON logging keeps `json.encode`; spec D224 (user selected recommended of 4). Rejected: a one-function `std/strconv`, JSON-style quoting for diagnostics, or leaving the private helpers separate. |
| 2026-10-29 | Q72: lint AST ownership (F5) | **Keep the generated public `lint.*` tree through pre-1.0**, with `selfhost/ast/ast.vs` as the maintained source and `TestLintAPI` guarding generated output; likely promote the tree to public `std/ast` at 1.0, with the final contract reviewed before release; spec D225. Rejected for now: a separate lint-owned schema and adapter; promoting `std/ast` before 1.0. |
| 2026-10-29 | Q73: public configuration schema for tools (F6) | **Publish TOML-backed `std/settings` schema primitives** for independent tools; keep compiler-owned manifest keys closed, custom lint rules severity-only, and formatter output canonical; spec D226 (user selected option 2 of 3). Rejected: keeping settings private; adding per-rule lint options. |

| 2026-10-29 | Q74: template processing and declaration | **Two lists and contextual `template fun`, exactly two parameters**, D227 (user selected recommended options). Rejected for now: builder protocol, `Template<V>` argument, extra defaulted parameters, retaining `@template`. Built and verified on macOS. |
| 2026-10-29 | Q75: HTML/URL module ownership and scope | **Standalone `html` and `url`, no template module; full contextual HTML including JavaScript/CSS; general absolute/relative URLs**, D228 (user chose full HTML over recommended initial subset; general URL scope as recommended). Concrete public APIs and module implementations remain open. |
| 2026-10-29 | Q76: trusted template literal pieces | **Tag-only invocation**, D227 addendum (user selected recommended of 3). Ordinary calls and function-value references are compile-time errors, including qualified names and calls inside holes. Rejected: defining-module-only calls and unrestricted ordinary calls. Built and verified on macOS. |
| 2026-10-30 | Q78 + Q79: test expectations — roles and a message | **`expectEqual(expected, actual)`, expected first, as its own word; `expect(cond, "message")`, lazy, laid out like `assert`**, D229 (user over the recommended "a literal side of `==` is expected": "always expected first, so the function reads expect(expected, got)"; then 2a of 3 shapes; message: recommended of 3). Rejected: roles inferred inside `==`; `expect(expected, actual)` under one name (D179); a named `because:`; no message. |
| 2026-10-30 | Q80: floats within a tolerance | **`f64`/`f32` `isClose(other, relative:, absolute:)` in the prelude and the test word `expectNear(expected, actual, within:)`**, D230 (user: recommended A plus B). Rejected: only writing `(a - b).abs() <= tolerance`. |

## 11. Known limitations to revisit

- [ ] **F45/F46 path verification and throughput (built 2026-10-30).** UNC share
      roots with/without their final separator now compare as the same path,
      preserving sibling and root-kind rejection. All six module cases pass
      on macOS in debug/release. Run Windows/Linux and staticcheck too.
      The new 200,000-check POSIX containment benchmark measured 80.23 ms
      before and 78.22 ms after versus Go's 7.95/7.88 ms. Single runs do not
      establish a speed change; profile the roughly 10x common-path gap.
      No normalization output or symlink behavior changed.
      The first full Go run failed in driver (285.210 s), but the test identity
      was lost to output truncation. A complete JSON driver run passed after
      it. Do not label the initial failure pre-existing or fixed without a
      reproducer; retain structured logs on the next full gate to diagnose it.
      F47 now retains per-run JSON/stderr through the default macOS wrapper;
      one new uncached full-suite JSON run passed without reproducing the old
      failure. That is improved observability, not a flake fix. F46 profiles
      normalizer allocation and skips its old replace/split/join path only
      for already-normalized input: five million checks improve from
      1.346-1.379 s to 0.607-0.643 s (52-56% less time). The independent Go
      oracle covers 2,664 paths/eight roots in debug/release; drive/UNC/Unicode
      fallback and idempotence are pinned too. Remaining throughput and
      Windows/Linux/staticcheck verification are still open.
      The default wrapper's full gate passes on Go 1.27.1/Apple clang 21;
      retained JSON has 18 passing test-bearing packages and no failures.

- [ ] **F43 process metadata violates D18 (2026-10-30; Q77 parked).** POSIX
      argv[0], arguments and environment values containing byte 0xFF become
      invalid strings through `os.program`, `os.args` and `os.env`. Isolated
      debug/release probes both print `programValid=false`,
      `argumentValid=false`, `environmentPresent=true environmentValid=false`.
      Unlike `os.run`, these accessors currently cannot throw; settle the
      failure policy before changing signatures, defaults or startup behavior.
      No runtime change has been made for this finding.

- [ ] **F42 subprocess UTF-8 platform/analysis verification (2026-10-30).**
      Shared Windows/POSIX output finalization validates captured stdout and
      stderr before constructing strings; malformed data returns InvalidData.
      Eighteen debug/release scenarios pass on macOS, covering empty/Unicode/
      NUL output, merge/inherit, nonzero status and split sequences. Run the
      same portable regression and full gates on Windows/Linux, and staticcheck.
      Paired capture benchmark: 208.65-217.74 ms before, 206.46-222.06 ms after;
      checksums agree and no performance change is claimed. No binary-capture
      API or process-option change was introduced.
      Captured output still has no size ceiling; bounded capture needs a
      separate public-API decision rather than an implicit limit in this fix.

- [ ] **F40/F41/F44 regex verification and remaining throughput (2026-10-30).**
      Nullable greedy repetitions retain participating empty captures; all 31
      module tests and 952 independent Go-oracle results per debug/release
      profile pass on macOS. F41 profiles scratch allocation and lazily reserves
      small local stacks without capping growth: 10 million searches improve
      from 2.020-2.044 s to 1.742-1.767 s (13-15% less time). The short benchmark
      remains noisy: 8.62-14.50 ms versus Go's 3.34-4.73 ms for 50,000 searches.
      Keep profiling the remaining allocation/throughput gap rather than
      treating it as closed. Run Windows/Linux and staticcheck too. Whole-match
      checksums and separate capture tests pass; no API or shared cache changed.
      F44 fixes negated POSIX class parsing, including fold-before-complement
      and outer negation. Literal/evaluator checks pass. The compilation-only
      benchmark measured 10.81 ms before and 10.65 ms after for 3,000 patterns;
      these single runs do not establish a speed change.

- [ ] **F11/F13/F10 selfhost platform and analysis verification (2026-10-29).**
      The AST printer is split into shared helpers plus declaration, type,
      statement and expression `extend Printer` files; lexer diagnostic
      selection uses `when`. All selfhost oracles pass on macOS, with a
      release-built frontend and byte-identical Go output. Run Windows/Linux
      gates and staticcheck too. No AST API or dump format changed; other F10
      review areas remain open.
      F11 derives formatter/linter choice mappings from enums with existing
      APIs; 18 additional manifest-oracle cases pin accepted values, defaults
      and refusal diagnostics, including the internal line-ending sentinel.

- [ ] **F9/F39 platform and analysis verification (built 2026-10-29).** The
      original eleven F9 modules all have local suites; 66 new tests cover
      encodings (19), I/O (9), random (10), OS (8), TLS (9) and FFI (11).
      All are discovered by the standard driver gate; native integration
      remains. Run Windows/Linux gates, Linux sanitizers and staticcheck.
      F39 fixes concurrent POSIX OpenSSL initialization with `pthread_once`;
      macOS thread/GC stress passes. HTTP sanity benchmark: 413.71 ms before,
      412.05 ms after, within noise (not a TLS initialization benchmark).
      F38 random benchmark: 18.33-22.47 ms before, 19.17-20.72 ms after,
      matching Go checksums; no speedup is claimed for either fix.
      F10's config-only branch cleanup also passes 30 debug/release tests;
      its HTTP client cleanup passes all 186 HTTP tests in both profiles.
      HTTP benchmark: 417.16 ms before, 411.42 ms after, within noise.
      Cross-platform verification remains outstanding, and the other F10
      areas remain open in the review plan.

- [ ] **F8 platform/analysis verification (built 2026-10-29).** Prelude codec
      organization, the 69-case prelude suite, and foreign std test isolation
      in library checks/phase dumps/editor indexing pass full macOS build/vet/
      tests. Run Windows/Linux gates too; staticcheck was not installed on this
      Mac. No public codec or collection API changed.

- [ ] **Templates D227/D228 (decided 2026-10-29).** D227 is built: contextual
      modifier, old-form diagnostic/fix, tag-only checking and Go/selfhost/tooling
      parity pass on macOS. Windows/WSL verification is outstanding.
      Implement standalone `std/url` (general
      references, resolution, component-safe construction) and `std/html`
      (contextual HTML, JavaScript and CSS handling, never generic escaping as
      a fallback). Q76's literal trust boundary is enforced; concrete
      error/conversion and HTTP integration contracts remain open.

- [ ] **Public `std/ast` direction for Veles 1.0 (D225).** Before the 1.0 API freeze, decide whether to promote the generated lint AST view to a public AST module, including the node, token-kind, file and span contracts. The user favors this direction; it is not a finalized API commitment.
- [ ] **`lazy val` left over (D210, plan B29 part 2 built 2026-10-27).**
      (a) D210 says the ready check is hoisted out of a loop; it is not: an
      acquire load is never hoisted, and a monotonic load plus a fence on
      the slow path would be, at the price of a fence on ARM. Measured
      today: a read is ~10% over a plain module `val` when the read is the
      whole of a non-inlined function (`bench/lazyval`), less inside a
      loop body that does work.
- [ ] **Allocation hints left over (D213, plan B31 built 2026-10-27).**
      (a) Sites are checked against the generated code by statement line,
      the line table's granularity: two sites on one line are not told
      apart. Expression-level line entries in codegen would make the check
      exact (and help a debugger). (b) Other editors than VS Code need the
      `allocationHints` initialization option documented in their setup.
      (c) `@noAlloc` with an inferred effect is to be asked again (D213's
      rejected-for-now) now that the hints exist.
- [ ] **`const struct` left over (D212, plan B30 built 2026-10-27).**
      (a) Removing `const` from a public type is an API break that
      `veles apidiff` should report; the tool does not exist yet (plan G1,
      on S12's interface files). (b) Sharing a large const struct by
      address with no defensive copy: immutability of the value is not
      enough, since a `var` holding it can be reassigned during the call;
      D121's caller rule already lends what is safe. Measure before
      anything more. (c) `selfhost/` could declare its tokens, spans and
      AST nodes `const struct` (D212's self-hosting note) once the AST
      settles (S13).
- [ ] **A nullable niche in `string` and in sealed values** (parked with
      numbers, `veles-layout-spec.md` §5.5): a `string?` or sealed `T?`
      still carries a flag word. A string niche needs every empty string to
      have a non-null pointer (C-produced ones included); a sealed one needs
      1-based tags at every tag site, for 2 uses in `selfhost`.
- [x] **Value layout left over (plan B32 step 6), done 2026-10-27:** exact
      GC maps for sealed and error payloads (the union of the variants'
      pointer words); `@align(n)` kept inside tuples, nullables and sealed
      or error payloads (D120 note); `noalias nocapture readonly` on
      borrowed big parameters (D121 note). Found on the way and fixed: a
      suspending function's frame was allocated 8-aligned while LLVM laid
      it out for an over-aligned local, so a release build crashed on a
      large `@align(64)` local kept across a suspension (frames now come
      at `llvm.coro.align`; golden `overalign` runs it in release).
- [ ] **`http.serve`: `idleTimeout` also bounds a new connection's first
      request** (`std/http/http.vs` `requestLine`; found 2026-10-27 when
      `std/http`'s "closed while idle is replaced" test failed once in the
      full WSL suite: with `idleTimeout: 100ms` the fresh connection the
      client retried on was closed before its request arrived). Go keeps
      the two apart (`ReadHeaderTimeout` for the first request,
      `IdleTimeout` between requests). The test's margins were widened
      (400 ms / 1.2 s); whether the server should have a separate
      first-request limit is a public API question (`Limits`) for
      `veles-decide`.
- [x] **Fixed 2026-10-27 (Windows): a string spanning lines kept the
      file's `\r`.** With `core.autocrlf`, `examples/todo` printed `\r\n` in
      its help text (a plain `"…"` over several lines; text blocks already
      dropped the `\r`), and `veles fmt` copied the `\r` of a literal into
      its LF output, so `TestCorpus` flagged the CRLF checkouts of
      `examples/regex` and `examples/todo`. Both lexers (Go and `selfhost/`)
      now read `\r\n` inside a string as `\n`; the formatter's `raw` writes
      LF. Pinned by `TestStringLineBreaksIgnoreCRLF` and `TestLineEndings`.
- [x] **Fixed 2026-10-27: `veles fmt` was not idempotent on a one-line
      receiver block holding a `when`** (`A { when (0) { () => 0 } }`,
      found by `FuzzFormat`): the first run kept the block on one line with
      the `when` broken inside it, the second expanded it. `receiverBlock`
      now falls back to a block when a statement prints over several lines.
      Pinned by `TestReceiverBlockWithWhenIsIdempotent` and the fuzz seed.

- [x] **Layout bugs found 2026-10-26 (veles-layout-spec.md §4).** All four
      fixed the same day. (1) `var x: T` with no value in a function was
      zero-filled with no definite-assignment check: `var p: *Named` passed
      as `(*Named)?` read as `null`, and `var xs: List<i64>; xs.len()`
      exited 255 with no message — now refused (D214, plan B32 step 2,
      `sema/testdata/conform/D214-definite-assignment.vs`). (2) a constant
      table of an `@align` or packed struct failed in clang — constants are
      built from the struct's slots (`structSlots`), pinned by
      `TestConstantTablesOfPaddedStructs`; (3) the memory class starts at
      128 bytes as D121 says; (4) `types.PayloadOffset` is the one payload
      offset (GC descriptors, the fail-fast launch), and the runtime stops
      with an internal error if the compiler passes another.
- [x] **Fixed 2026-10-26: run-time crashes on macOS arm64.** `veles test
std/compress` exited 255, and `TestHashFlooding`,
      `TestReactorPollFallback` (segfaults), `TestStdRegexUnitTests` ("list
      was frozen by freeze()"), `TestTLSServer`,
      `TestReactorManyConnections` and `TestStdHttpUnitTests` failed. Cause:
      a dead large object's span is given back with `os_decommit`, and a
      later span carved from that place is taken to read as zeros
      (`new_span`, the `virgin` slots); Darwin's `MADV_DONTNEED` keeps the
      bytes, so new objects started with stale words — a list header with
      its frozen bit set, stale pointers. On macOS `os_decommit` now maps
      fresh pages over the range (`mmap` `MAP_FIXED`). Pinned by
      `TestReleasedMemoryReadsAsZeros` (fails without the fix); `go test
  ./...` green on this Mac (the Unicode tables regenerated the same day,
      with Go pinned to 1.27.1).
      Linux (`MADV_DONTNEED` zero-fills private anonymous pages) and Windows
      (`MEM_DECOMMIT`) were not affected.
- [x] **Fixed 2026-10-26 (plan B29 part 1):** `sema/check_fn.go`
      `checkGlobal` refuses a module or `static val` whose type is not
      `Sendable` (an executor's state is exempt), with a hint per kind:
      immutable collection, `trait X : Sendable`, `Mutex`, executor. Pinned
      by `TestConcurrencyRules` (six cases) and `TestConcurrencyAccepted`.
      Nothing in std, examples, selfhost or docs hit it;
      `TestPrivateMembers`'s fixture had a racy `static val` and now uses a
      `static fun`. Docs: chapter 12, chapter 14 and the error reference.
      **A module `val` of a mutable reference type races (found 2026-10-26,
      veles-patterns.md F1).** `val cache = MutableMap<i64, i64>()` at module
      level compiles, and four tasks calling `cache.set` hang or segfault under
      `VELES_THREADS=8`. `sema/check_fn.go` refuses an unsynchronized module
      `var` only. The global-`val` receiver check (`sema/receivers.go`, the
      `RecvPlace` case) does not see handle receivers (`MutableMap`,
      `MutableList`, `StringBuilder`). Fix per D66/D150: a module `val` and a
      `static val` have to have a `Sendable` type, and the error suggests
      `Mutex(value: …)`, an immutable collection or an executor. Migrate std
      and examples if any of them hit it.
- [x] **Decided and built 2026-10-26 (D66 addendum v0.114; user,
      recommended of 4): no module-level `var` at all.** A `var` of a
      synchronized type is an error with a fix that writes `val`, and the
      message names the type's write (`set`/`store`/`reset`). Assigning a
      global `val`, its field, or calling a mutating method on it no longer
      says "declare it 'var'"; executor state still does (D150). Pinned by
      `TestConcurrencyRules` (five cases) and `TestModuleVarOfLockFix`. The
      tree had no such `var`. **A module `var` of a synchronized type can be
      reassigned (found 2026-10-26, building the above).** `var hits = Atomic(value: 0)` is
      accepted, as D66 says ("a module-level `var` whose type is not
      synchronized" is the error). But `hits = Atomic(value: 5)` from a task
      then writes the global's slot with no lock, while other tasks read it.
      Refusing the assignment, or refusing `var` for these types ("use
      `val`: the lock is inside"), changes D66's text, so it needs a decision
      (veles-decide).

- **B19 / D157 (implemented 2026-10-22; verified on Windows and Linux with
  the B22 runs, 2026-10-23; staticcheck reports nothing new).**
  Result and task interpolation is rejected in strings, format specs and
  template values; explicit `Result.toString()` remains available. Checker,
  CLI repair/debug/release, LSP and migrated examples/docs are covered.
  The specified generic limit remains: a parameter declared `T` can be
  interpolated when instantiated as a Result; direct parameter coverage
  pins this exception, not a generic-effect or wrapper analysis.
- [x] **Fixed 2026-10-31 (plan N1c): `TestLoopsAreCancellationPoints`
      under load.** Two of its checks measured time against a margin. Case 3:
      `withTimeout(5 ms)` around a lock region of 30 M iterations reports a
      timeout only if the deadline's timer fired before the region ended
      (`veles_deadline_end` returns `fired`); a loaded 8-thread release run
      could finish the region (~30 ms) first. The region now lasts 250 ms
      by a stopwatch. Case 2 (with one thread too) asked that a ticker
      sleeping 50 ms beside a suspending loop ran "within 150 ms": with
      every core busy the OS kept the only thread off for longer (2 of 50
      runs on Windows under a 32-thread CPU burner). It now asks whether
      the loop was still going when the ticker woke, which no amount of
      load changes. 50 runs under that burner: no failure.
- [x] **Fixed: `TestLessCeremony` flake under load (2026-10-22).** The
      `stopped()` half was fixed in 689bca8 (the loop waits until a second
      call has started). 2026-10-31 (plan N1c): `runAhead` had the same
      kind of assumption — that calls 2 and 3 started within call 1's 5 ms
      sleep; call 1 now waits (up to 2 s) until three have started. 50 runs
      on Windows under a 32-thread CPU burner: no failure.
      Original report: a full WSL run
      of the `macos` branch failed it at release/4 threads: `stopped()`
      printed `every started call closed: false`. Five isolated reruns
      passed. The check `started.load() >= 2` assumes a second
      `mapConcurrent` call has started before the loop breaks on the first
      value, which scheduling does not promise (the test's own comment says
      so); `started == closed` is the property that matters. Decide whether
      to drop the `>= 2` half or make the first call wait for a second to
      start, and confirm `started == closed` never fails on its own.
- [ ] **Test-suite wall time, what is left (2026-10-22).** The compiler is
      now built once per test binary and doc blocks and examples run in
      parallel (docs 400–600 s → 41 s, examples ~300 s → 31 s, selfhost
      234 s → 55 s on Windows). Still slow, measured on Windows the same
      day: (1) `driver` was 506–586 s with all 116 tests serial; the ~40
      that only build and compare now run in parallel (`TestFreeze`'s
      subtests too) and it takes ~300 s. Nearly all of that is the
      serial phase: the timing- and thread-sensitive runtime tests
      (`runtime_test.go` alone ~150 s, `TestStdHttpUnitTests`,
      `TestLoopsAreCancellationPoints`), and tests that set the
      environment, swap stdio (`capture`, `runTestDir` — guarded by
      `serialOnly`) or set `runtimeTestFlags`. Shrinking it means making
      those programs cheaper to build (3) or running each with its own
      stdio and env instead of the process's. (2) Done 2026-10-23:
      `sema` `TestCheckEveryPrefix` re-parsed the embedded std for every
      line of every example (`fuzz` 181 s, `httpd` 100 s), as the language
      server did on every keystroke; now the embedded std is parsed once per
      process (`sema/stdparse.go`) and each load gets its own deep copy
      (`ast.Clone`, generated from `ast/ast.go` by `ast/internal/clonegen`),
      because the checker rewrites the tree it is given (D158 bare members,
      D176 decorators) — see "Checking the embedded std once" below for what
      is left. `go test ./sema` 384 s → 168 s on Windows. (3) Done
      2026-10-23: codegen keeps only the definitions and private constants
      reachable from what is named from outside the module (`veles_main`,
      `veles_init_globals`, `extern "C"` exports, non-private globals),
      following every `@name` in calls, function values, closures, coroutine
      frames and method tables (`codegen/llvm/prune.go`, fixture
      `golden/reach`). Hello world 540 → 4 `define`s, clang ~450 → ~245 ms;
      `httpd` 2156 → 822, clang 2.9 → 1.4 s; `./docs ./examples` 59 → 32 s
      (`bench/results.md`). With all three, `go test ./...` takes 228 s on
      Windows (it timed out at 10 minutes before) and 212 s on Linux with
      build and vet; `driver` (224 s) and `sema` (123 s) are the long poles.

- [ ] **Test tooling, left over (2026-10-30: expect's parts, run-time test
      options, the program cache, CI and `-short`).** (1) A failed `expect`
      shows no parts for a call through a function value or a trait object,
      one with named, spread or defaulted arguments (only arguments that are
      plain local names are shown then), `is`, `?:`, or a nested comparison's
      literal side; a value is printed whole, however long; a multi-line text
      on the `left:`/`right:` lines still breaks their indentation (the diff
      under them is aligned). (2) The program cache does not serve `veles
      build` or a package with native libraries (their files are not in the
      key), and has no switch to turn it off; `veles test --format json` and
      a VS Code test explorer are not built. (3) `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
      and `nightly.yml` were written but never run: the MSYS2 clang install
      on the Windows runner, the 30-minute bound and the nightly `-run`
      selection are unverified; CI checks out Windows with
      `core.autocrlf=false`, so it does not reproduce the CRLF item below.
      (5) D229/D230 as built: `expectEqual` takes no message (`expect(a ==
      b, "…")` does) and shows no parts beyond its two sides and their calls'
      arguments; `expectNear` has no relative tolerance (`expect(a.isClose(b))`
      gives one) and no `f32`/`f64` mixing; `isClose`'s `f32` default
      (relative `1.0e-6`) was the implementer's choice, not asked.
      (6) Fixed 2026-10-31 (plan N1c): `driver TestStdFsUnitTests` failed
      once while two `go test ./driver` runs overlapped — every `std/fs` test
      wrote into one fixed `$TMP/veles-fs-test`, so two runs removed each
      other's files. The `fs`, `config` and `http` (gzip, files, multipart)
      tests now put the process id in their directory's name. Left: those
      directories stay in the temp directory after a run (std has no
      recursive remove, and tests have no teardown).
      (4) `-short` on macOS: `go test ./...` ~290 s → 187 s, `driver` 288 s
      → 101 s (on Windows, as rebuilt 2026-10-31 with 9 tests in
      `skipInShort`: `driver` 231 s → 154 s); `docs` (100 s) is now the long pole of the quick tier and the
      driver's serial phase (tests that capture stdio) is unchanged.

- [ ] **Windows Defender quarantines the compiler (2026-10-31).** `go
      build` of this tree at `C:\Users\lahpo\Code\veles` produced a binary
      Defender removed as `Trojan:Win32/Bearfoos.A!ml` (a machine-learning
      verdict, a known false positive class for Go binaries); `go test
      ./docs` and anything else that builds the compiler then fails with
      "contains a virus or potentially unwanted software". The same sources
      built from another directory, or with `GOFLAGS=-trimpath`, pass, so
      it follows the bytes (embedded source paths), not the code. Open:
      report the false positive to Microsoft, and decide whether release
      builds use `-trimpath` (and signing) so users do not meet it.

- [ ] **Runtime object cache races on Windows (2026-10-31).** On the first
      `go test ./...` after a runtime change (a new cache directory under
      `%LOCALAPPDATA%\veles\rt\`), parallel builds failed to link the freshly
      installed `veles_ffi.o`: `ld: cannot find …veles_ffi.o: Permission
      denied` (codegen golden `cede`), and twice `0-bit reloc in dll` /
      `local symbol (NULL) has no section` (examples `listpatterns`,
      `packages/app`). All three passed once the cache was warm.
      `runtimeObjects` (driver.go) compiles to a private name and renames,
      but on Windows a linker opening the object can still collide with
      another build's rename over it. Not investigated; a lock file around
      installing the directory, or installing it whole by renaming a
      directory, are the first candidates.

- [ ] **Checking the embedded std once, not per load (2026-10-23).** With
      parsed std cached (above), checking is what the language server and
      `sema` tests pay for: an edit of `examples/httpd/main.vs` (load +
      `CheckIndex`, as `lsp/server.go` `analyze` does) is ~9 ms of loading and
      ~590 ms of checking on Windows, and `TestCheckEveryPrefix/httpd` spends
      12 of its 31 CPU-seconds in `Check`, most of the rest in the GC it
      drives. The checker cannot keep checked std modules between loads as it
      stands: it is whole-program — every round of the effect fixpoint
      re-checks every body, std's included, into a fresh `Program` with fresh
      instance tables and variable numbering; generic std bodies are
      instantiated with the program's types, and their effects follow the
      caller's arguments (D116); D158 rewrites a method body against the
      receiver's member set, which a program's own `implement` of a trait on
      a std type extends. Caching it is separate compilation of std: freeze,
      after checking std alone, per-module summaries (scopes, signatures,
      inferred effects, impl and trait tables, `types` values with an
      identity every program shares read-only) and the non-generic HIR;
      instantiate generic std bodies per program in a layer of its own;
      make every checker table that std entries live in a per-program
      overlay over the frozen one; give HIR variable and loop numbers a
      per-module base so frozen bodies keep theirs. A design change of the
      checker, not a cache; measure first how much of `Check` is std bodies
      (`--timings` reports check time per module).

- [x] **Fixed 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-31 (plan N1d).** The lexer
      and formatter change of 2026-10-27 ("a string spanning lines kept the
      file's `\r`") fixed this: with both files checked out CRLF, `format
      TestCorpus` and `examples` `todo`/`regex` pass, pinned by
      `TestLineEndings` and `TestStringLineBreaksIgnoreCRLF`, which feed
      CRLF sources. `.gitattributes` was left alone. Original report — a
      fresh Windows checkout failed three tests (found 2026-10-23, by
      both the codegen and the std-parse sessions): with
      `core.autocrlf=true`, `examples/regex/main.vs` and
      `examples/todo/main.vs` check out with CRLF (the main checkout has
      them with LF): `format` `TestCorpus` says both are not formatted, and
      `examples/todo` prints its multi-line usage string with `\r\n`, which
      does not match `expected.txt`. Linux is green. Either the formatter
      and multi-line string literals should not depend on the line ending a
      Windows checkout gives a source, or `.gitattributes` should pin `*.vs`
      to `eol=lf`.

- [ ] **`cede` and the loop yield, left over (D197 built and D145 measured,
      plans F20 and F21, 2026-10-27; numbers in `bench/results.md`).**
      (a) Not run: `driver/cede_test.go` on macOS, and the Linux sanitizer
      build (`go test ./driver -run TestCede -sanitize` in WSL).
      (b) A yield under contention: eight tasks ceding at every round cost
      ~5.3 µs a cede, against ~2.2 µs for Go's `runtime.Gosched` (~90 ns
      against 85 ns on one thread). Both go through a shared queue under a
      lock; ours is a spin lock that falls back to `SwitchToThread`. `cede`
      is meant to be rare; measure before changing where a yield is queued
      (it decides who may run next).
      (c) A suspending function's loop that D218 does not chunk (its body
      calls, or it runs on a condition) stores what it keeps to the
      coroutine frame on every round, because the back edge's yield path is
      a suspension point: measured before D218 at half the plain twin's
      speed (1723 against 865 ps a round). A chunked loop no longer pays it
      (114 against 107 ps): the inner loop holds no suspension point.
      Decision-free codegen work for the rest (spill only on the cold path).
      (d) The poll itself blocked the loop optimizations: D218, built
      2026-10-28 (plan F22).
      (e) A plain function's loop cannot yield (D145, by design): beside one,
      a ticker waits for the whole loop (315 ms of 315 at one worker). The
      remedy stays `scope(on:)`/`blocking` or making the function suspend.
      (f) Generators (I1) and call-site "may suspend" hints are not part of
      D197.

- **macOS validation follow-up (A7, 2026-10-21):** Intel Macs are untested;
  Windows and Linux were not available to rerun this change. Staticcheck was
  skipped at the user's request. Go 1.27's Unicode 17 tables fail the generated
  selfhost/regex table checks; Go 1.23.2 passes without changing language data.
  Repeating the 302-connection reactor stress can exhaust Darwin's ephemeral
  ports (`EADDRNOTAVAIL`); use bounded socket runs and socket-free programs for
  sustained thread/GC stress. macOS still refuses non-default thread priority
  and CPU affinity (D143), and marks in the GC pause (F18).

- **A `List` extension method called on a `MutableList` sees the mutable list as `this` (found 2026-10-21, while building D196).** `extend<T> List<T> { fun me(): List<T> => this }` returns an alias: `val a = m.me(); m.set(0, 99)` changes `a`. It predates D196, which only closed the new way in (a window of `this` is a copy in a program's own methods; std's windows have `MutableList` versions). The rule that closes it for good is D63's for a receiver: a method that lets `this` escape (returns it, stores it, passes it on as a `List`) is refused on a mutable receiver, or the receiver is copied; the escape summary of D188 (`sema/escape.go`) is the tool. Not built.
- **D196 (built 2026-10-21): a window keeps its whole buffer alive.** A small `slice`, `take` or `drop` of a large immutable `List` retains the large storage until the window is dropped; `toList()` copies it free. No lint warns about it, and the research options C (a view type) and D (a mutable window) are not built. A `windowed(n)` over a long list is still eager: one small header per window.
- **D189–D194 (built 2026-10-20) — what is left.** (1) ~~The selfhost lexer/parser/AST do not know the new syntax~~ — mirrored 2026-10-20 (compound tokens placed after the keywords in both enums, `in`/`!in`, `InExpr`, nested variants hoisted in `parseFile`; `loop (val x = e)` needed nothing; `sema/testdata/conform/D189-syntax.vs` pins it under the parse gate). Left: the selfhost formatter does not exist yet, so nothing mirrors the Go formatter's nested-variant printing. (2) `i in 0..<xs.len()` does not yet establish a bounds fact (D62). (3) A string literal over 16 bytes on the right of `in` is a call, not comparisons. (4) `>>=` after a generic close needs a space (`List<List<i64>> = x`). (5) ~~The 71 `val x = f() ?: break` loops are not migrated~~ — migrated 2026-10-21 by tool, with the rest: 24 loop heads (only where the `val … ?: break` line is first in a bare `loop {`; the others stay), 97 `in`/range tests (single-line chains only; multi-line chains stay), 50 compound assignments, 76 `substring(…) ?: ""` → `slice`, and the variants of seven files moved into their sealed traits (`selfhost/ast/ast.vs` 89, `std/regex/parse.vs`, `std/toml/value.vs`, `std/prelude/codec.vs`, `std/otel/attr.vs`, `selfhost/pkg/dependency.vs`, `selfhost/formatter/options.vs`). Left on the old form on purpose: `examples/` (they teach both spellings; the docs quote them). The `slice` change differs from `substring ?: ""` only for bounds that were invalid (clamped now, empty before). (6) No quick fix moves a top-level variant into its trait. (7) Open: list-slice sharing (`veles-research-views.md`), generators (`veles-research-generators.md`), `skipWhile`/`indexWhere` names, Q57 (panic marker).
- **A scheduling-dependent line in `TestLessCeremony` (seen 2026-10-20 and again 2026-10-21, WSL; 2 of 8 repeated runs the second time, `-count=8`).** `stopped()` prints `every started call closed: false` once in three full Linux runs of the package (4 threads, tests in parallel); alone it passed 25 of 25, and it passes on Windows and on the other full runs. Its condition is `started == closed && started >= 2`, read right after the `break`: either the first result came back before a second worker had started (the `>= 2` half is the test's own assumption), or a cancelled child had not closed yet (a real ordering question for `mapConcurrent`'s close). Not told apart; to find out, run the test in a loop under load with the two halves printed apart.

- **Empty values own no storage (D187, built 2026-10-20, `TestEmptyListNoStorage`).** Mutable lists (`cap 0, data NULL` until the first push), mutable maps and sets (no arrays until the first insert), `StringBuilder` (its list), an unbuffered `Channel` (no buffer), the immutable `[]` / `[:]` / `List<T>()` / `Set<T>()` (one shared constant per element type, no allocation), and `""` pieces of a concatenation (dropped at compile time) are done; `Deque`/`PriorityQueue` were already lazy; `bench/emptycoll` 205 → ~70 ms. Also done: `.toList()` of an empty list is the shared constant; `a + b` with an empty side returns the other string when no piece is a formatted number (a concatenation with an integer piece copies: the number is a temporary buffer until copied); a map of up to 8 entries has no hash index. Left: a `MutableList`/`MutableMap` header is a heap object unless it never leaves its function (D188: then a frame slot — `emptycoll` 70 → ~27 ms, level with Go's 19–36 ms); a `var` collection variable, one that becomes another struct's field, and anything in a function that can suspend keep the heap header; `List.rawData` of an empty list is NULL (an FFI call passing it with length 0 must accept that). `lazy` fields are not added (user, 2026-10-20). The GC wipe probe (`veles_gc_test_wipe`) now scrubs the stack and retries, because its first probe survived a conservative scan once the allocation pattern shifted.

- **`std/version` (D186, built 2026-10-19).** Not built: hyphen ranges (`1.2.3 - 2.3.4`), a minimal-version-selection resolver on top of it (the loader's work), `Version`/`Constraint` as codec values, constraints in `[dependencies]`. Error text uses shared `fmt.quote` (F4, D224). `Version.parse` and the bootstrap compiler's `ParseVersion` agree on every case in `TestR1Manifest`.

- **The `extend` clause of a `use` (D185, built 2026-10-19).** Not built: the editor's auto-import of an extension name (it offers the module, not the clause), `veles check --fix` writing the clause for a missing extension, a warning for an `extend { f }` name the file never calls, and the module's hover marking which extensions the clause brings (the inlay hint at the end of a use line already follows the clause: none without it, only the brought types with it). The selfhost parser and printer read the clause; the lint API (`std/lint`) carries `extend` and `extendNames` on a use spec.

- **Unicode tables (D223; F3 built 2026-10-29).** The generated `std/unicode/tables.vs` is checked by `std/unicode_test.go` against the pinned Go Unicode data and bootstrap lexer predicates; regex and selfhost use the shared module. **Diagnostic quoting (F4, D224) is built:** `std/toml`, `std/version` and `selfhost/text` delegate to `fmt.quote`; `std/log` retains JSON encoding. `selfhost.TestP3Quote` pins byte-identical output against Go's `strconv.Quote`.

- **Self-hosted manifest reader (plan R1, `selfhost/pkg/`, `settings/`, `formatter/`, `linter/`, built and redesigned 2026-10-19).** Compared with the Go reader on every `veles.toml` of the repository and ~130 cases (`TestR1Manifest`), and the lint registry (`TestR1Lints`); the lint checks themselves are R8. `selfhost/text/suggest.vs` differs from the bootstrap compiler's where that uses the Unicode tables: a candidate's first character counts as a letter by the identifier table (`isIDStart`, XID_Start) rather than `unicode.IsLetter`, and `toLower` is the prelude's. Not ported: the checker's own hints (`unknownNameHint`, member names) — R4. Version comparison and `MajorID` (`Compare`) come with the dependency resolver.

- **`std/toml` (D183, built 2026-10-19).** Not built: typed `toml.decode<T>` through `Decodable` and the `Encoder` side (writing TOML); conversion of dates to `Timestamp`/`Duration` (they are kept as the text written, checked for form and range); keys come back sorted, not in file order; TOML 1.1 additions (`\xHH`, newlines in inline tables, optional seconds) are not read; the error texts of the full language are not pinned by a corpus (the strict mode is, byte for byte, by `TestR1Toml`). The Go `sema` keeps its own `toml.go` until the Go side is retired.

- **The HIR bridge of the self-hosted checker (Q-S1, decided 2026-10-19) is scaffolding and must be cleaned up.** The readable HIR text and its Go reader exist so a Veles `sema` can feed the Go `codegen`; when `codegen` is ported they go, or become the module interface-file format (S12). Do not let the format grow features of its own.

- **`std/regex` and the `/…/` literal (D182, built 2026-10-19, `\p{…}`, the `u` flag and `backtracking: true` the same day).** Not built: **typed groups** (`Regex<(date: string, …)>` from the literal's named groups) — a later extension; the **full case folding** of Unicode (`ß` against `SS`; the `u` flag folds one code point to one), and `\p{…}` under `(?i)` is not folded; **possessive and atomic groups** and Perl's recursion, conditionals and `\G`, which the exhaustive engine does not read. The **exhaustive engine** has no step limit: a hostile pattern or text can run for ever, which is why it is opt-in by name and a literal cannot ask for it; a lookbehind tries every earlier start, so it is linear in the text per check. **Unicode tables** add 139 KB of source to every program that imports `regex` (parsed on each build; nothing is compiled in unless used); the data follows the Go toolchain's Unicode version, and the generator test fails when it moves. **Performance** (Windows, `-O2`, 100 000 log lines of 75 bytes, 2026-10-19): the capturing line pattern 90 ms against Go's `regexp` 52 ms (1.7×), `\w+` over every line 247 ms against 165 ms (1.5×); started at 47× and 12× with an allocating thread per step; the backtracker fuses one-character loops, marks only at jump targets and tests ASCII sets with a mask. Left: no DFA (a lazy DFA for `test`/`find` without groups would close most of it), the backtracker allocates its visited set per search, a literal's compiled value is kept in a `Mutex`-guarded cache in `std/regex` (about 100 ns per evaluation) instead of a global the compiler would emit once per literal, and a text over 262 144 / program-size bytes runs on the slower threads. The regex rule in the lexer divides after `)`, `]`, `}` and a value, as JavaScript does: `if (ok) /x/.test(s)` on one line is read as division (write braces); the LSP shows no hover on a literal and no group names; Linux (WSL) was not run for this change; the string extensions `text.replaceAll(re, replacement)` and `re.replaceAll(text, replacement)` are two spellings of one operation (kept on purpose).

- **Bootstrap fixpoint (plan S14, harness built 2026-10-19).** The gate, `TestSelfHostedCompiler`,
  skips: there is no Veles compiler to build yet (S11). Compared: the LLVM IR of the compiler's
  source in `--release`; not compared: the executables byte for byte (they carry the build
  directory and, on Windows, a timestamp), the output of running each stage over the whole
  corpus, and a build with the sanitizers. The harness assumes the stages speak
  `build . -o out --release [--emit-llvm]`; a Veles compiler with a different command line adapts
  `Compiler.Build`. No CI line yet, and not run on Linux.

- **Phase dumps (plan S10b, built 2026-10-19).** `--dump modules|scopes|decls|effects` and `--dump-spans` are the first cut the sema port's gates (R1–R3, R5) need. Not in them: `decls` shows the declarations the checker resolved — a type no body reached may show an empty field list — and leaves out type aliases' targets, annotations and their uses, executors and `static val`s; `scopes` lists names, kinds and visibility levels but not types (they are not resolved yet, by design); `effects` reports the concrete (instantiated) functions of the program, so a generic template that was never instantiated has no line, and the round count is the Go checker's (a port that reaches the same fixpoint in fewer rounds fails the gate on purpose — decide then whether that is a finding or a relaxation); `modules` shows imports as resolved for the package it was run on, not the dependency packages' own graphs (`Deps` of other packages are listed by module path only). The `hir` dump's header still says `suspends` twice for a suspending function (the signature's and the effect list's). Spans are start–end in one `line:col-line:col` form, columns in bytes; the dumps that print no span (`names`, `types`) have no spans flag. Linux not run.
- **Sema dumps (plan S10, first cut built 2026-10-19).** `veles check --dump names|types|hir`
  dumps the program _after_ lowering, not each phase separately: there is no dump of name
  resolution before type checking, of declarations before bodies, or of the effects pass on its
  own, which the sema port's phase gates (S11) will want. Not in the dumps: spans (a Veles
  checker is not compared on positions yet), generic _templates_ that were never instantiated
  (only the instances in the program), the checker's warnings and lint results, constant values
  folded by `const fun` beyond what the HIR holds, and the std symbols unless `--dump-std`.
  `names` is a symbol list, not the resolution of each use (that is the `Fn:` of a call in
  `hir`). The dump walks the HIR by reflection, so its text changes when an HIR field is
  added or renamed — regenerate the goldens with `go test ./sema -run TestDumps -update-dump`.
  The goldens cover three small programs (`sema/testdata/dump/`); a corpus-wide comparison
  (examples, std) and a check that the dump is the same on Linux were not done.

- **Skipping tests (D178, built 2026-10-18, plan B28).** Not built or not verified: a suite's `skip`/`skipIf` gate that _panics_ (not skips) is treated as "did not skip" — its tests run — instead of failing them with the panic; the gates of all suites run before the first test is queued, not lazily per suite (a nested suite's gate runs even when the outer one skipped); ~~`@SkipIf(build.target.os == …)` waits for `build.target`~~ — works since 2026-10-24 (`std/build`, plan C9 (a)); `@Skip`/`@SkipIf` take positional arguments (only these two annotations do — D174 requires names for the others; the user may want it general); LSP outline shows no entry for a suite's `skip` statements; `--fail-on-skip` is not repeated in the summary line; the Go driver tests' `t.Skip` conditions (clang, PostgreSQL) were not mirrored into Veles tests — no std test currently returns early for a missing tool.

- **Extension functions (D173, built 2026-10-17, plan B25).** Not built: extensions on a
  nullable receiver (`string?`), on a trait or `dyn` type, a pointer, a tuple or a function
  type (all answer "only named types can be extended"); `static fun` in a foreign extension
  (refused); an extension on an `Array<T, N>` does not see the read-only `List` methods'
  copy view that own `extend` blocks get (D121) — only `extend … Array` matches; a module
  reached through a facade's `public use` does not bring its extensions (only a direct
  `use` of the declaring module does); completion after a `.` offers the extension
  functions of every module of the package, imported or not (it filters by visibility, not
  by the file's imports); `veles doc` and the module interface do not list extensions as
  such; the ambiguity message offers no mechanical fix (there is no call form that names the
  module). A pipe operator `|>` was not added — re-open only if real code shows a gap (the
  value going to a non-first parameter).

- **Bare member names (D158, built 2026-10-17).** The rule is applied by a rewrite before
  a body is checked: a name the body binds anywhere as a local is never read as a member
  there (the reader gets "unknown name … `this.x`" where the local is not in scope, instead
  of the spec's shadowing warning, which is not built); hover does not say "field of
  `this`"; rename and references cover both spellings only because the rewritten node keeps
  its span. Not done: the lint preferring one spelling, and migrating std and `selfhost/` to
  bare names (left to the user, as the spec says); the module-level-name warning reports at
  the use, not at the member's declaration. Labels on tests (D170): tags for filtering
  (`--tag`) were rejected for now; take them up with `@slow`-style needs.
- **Format specs and strings (D171, D172, built 2026-10-17).** Not built: the spec type
  `e`, and a width or precision computed at run time (`${x:>${w}}`; refused with a
  message); a user type's own spec (every printing type takes fill/align/width of what it
  prints, nothing more); **locales in `std/fmt` — decimal commas, dates — a noted todo**
  (they touch `std/time`); `veles fmt` keeps a text block verbatim, so moving its opening
  to another indentation does not move the body (the margin stays correct, the block just
  looks unaligned); a raw backtick string cannot be tagged or hold a backtick; the editor
  grammar does not colour the spec; `std/fmt` is the first set (`thousands`, `bytes`,
  `percent`, `ordinal`, `duration`, `table`) — more when asked.
- **Linux (WSL)** was not run for the 2026-10-17 change (D170, B17, B20); run
  `wsl -d Ubuntu -- bash …/internal/wsl-test.sh`.
